Here, the second line holds because <sup>L</sup> = Ω (ϵ ∨ γ) s <sup>⋆</sup>−1 · d s <sup>⋆</sup>/2 log d ∨ d log d and the last line holds because Pr(E(ϵ)) ≤ d −s <sup>⋆</sup>/2 .

So far, we have obtained that on the event E(ϵ) ∩ E1,<sup>1</sup> ∩ E1,2, the following holds:

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g, \theta^* \rangle] \simeq \mathbb{E}_{w_l}[\langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] + E + R,$$

where |E| + |R| ≲ (|ρ|γ + d −1/2 ) s ⋆ given our configuration of L and Pr(E(ϵ) c ). On the other hand, provided that |ρ|γ ≫ d −1 , we have that

$$\begin{aligned} (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1/2})^{s^*} &= (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1/2})^{s^*-2} \cdot ((|\rho|\gamma)^2 + d^{-1} + 2 \cdot |\rho|\gamma \cdot d^{-1/2}) \\ &= (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1/2})^{s^*-2} \cdot |\rho|\gamma \cdot (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1} \cdot (|\rho|\gamma)^{-1} + 2d^{-1/2}) \\ &\ll (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1/2})^{s^*-2} \cdot |\rho|\gamma. \end{aligned}$$

Therefore, <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [⟨g, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩] is always the major term no matter whether s ⋆ is even or odd, and we have that

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g, \theta^* \rangle] \simeq \begin{cases} \gamma \rho \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + d^{-1/2})^{s^*-2} & \text{if } s^* \text{ is even;} \\ (\gamma |\rho| + d^{-1/2})^{s^*-1} & \text{if } s^* \text{ is odd.} \end{cases}$$

We now turn to the norm of <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [g]. We have already shown the projection of <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [g] onto θ ⋆ . Next, define P ⊥ θ <sup>⋆</sup> = I − θ ⋆ θ <sup>⋆</sup><sup>⊤</sup> as the projection matrix onto the orthogonal complement of the space spanned by θ ⋆ . Now, it follows from Eq. [\(H.6\)](#page-68-0) that

$$\begin{aligned} \|P_{\theta^*}^\perp \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[g]\|_2 &\leq \sum_{s \geq s^*} |\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_s(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)]| \cdot \left\| \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle^s \cdot w_l \right\|_2 \\ &\lesssim \sum_{s \geq s^*} \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle|^s. \end{aligned}$$

Here, the first inequality holds by noting that the second term in Eq. [\(H.6\)](#page-68-0) lies exactly along the direction of θ ⋆ and thus does not contribute to the norm of P ⊥ θ <sup>⋆</sup> <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [g], while for the first term in Eq. [\(H.6\)](#page-68-0), we use the triangle inequality and the fact that ∥P ⊥ θ <sup>⋆</sup> v∥<sup>2</sup> ≤ ∥v∥<sup>2</sup> for any v ∈ <sup>R</sup> d . In the second inequality, we also use the triangle inequality and the fact that ∥wl∥<sup>2</sup> = 1 for all l ∈ [L]. Here, the "≲" hides a constant that depends on the boundedness of <sup>E</sup>Q[ζs(y) · <sup>ψ</sup>ps+1(y)] as we have shown in the previous analysis.

Note that the term P s≥s ⋆ √ s+1 L P<sup>L</sup> <sup>l</sup>=1 |⟨w<sup>l</sup> , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩|s is already handled in Eq. [\(E.8\)](#page-40-0) and [\(E.9\)](#page-40-1) under the success of event E(ϵ) ∩ E1,1, on which we have

$$\|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[g]\|_2 - |\langle \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[g], \theta^* \rangle| \lesssim \sum_{s \geq s^*} \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle|^s \lesssim (|\rho|\gamma + d^{-1/2})^{s^*}.$$

Setting E<sup>1</sup> = E1,<sup>1</sup> ∩ E1,<sup>2</sup> gives the desired event.

*Proof of [Proposition E.4.](#page-33-0)* The polynomial-tail property allows us to control the fluctuation of the gradient estimator g in each direction at the level that is determined by the sample size n and the corresponding variance. To this end, we begin with calculating the variance of g along each direction.

Calculating the second moment. Given θ and θ ⋆ , recall that we consider the following d orthonormal directions:

$$\theta^*, v_2, v_3, \dots, v_d,$$

where we set v<sup>2</sup> = (θ − ⟨θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩θ ⋆ )/∥θ − ⟨θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩θ <sup>⋆</sup>∥<sup>2</sup> and v<sup>i</sup> for i ≥ 3 are orthogonal to θ ⋆ and v2. Our goal is to show that g has small variance on each of these directions.

As each sample (z<sup>i</sup> , yi) is independently drawn from <sup>P</sup><sup>θ</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> , we just need to consider the variance of

$$g_1 = \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L (\psi(y_1, \langle w_l, z_1 \rangle) \cdot z_1 - \hat{\psi}_1(y_1) \cdot w_l)$$

in the direction of v for v ∈ {θ ⋆ , v1, . . . , vd−1} as

$$\begin{aligned} \text{Var}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle] &= \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle^2] - \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle]^2 \\ &\leq \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle^2]. \end{aligned}$$

From this we see that it suffices to bound the second moment of ⟨g1, v⟩, which is given by

$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle^2] &\lesssim \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l, l'=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_{l'}, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2] + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l, l'=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} \left[ \hat{\psi}_1(y)^2 \langle w_l, v \rangle \langle w_{l'}, v \rangle \right] \\ &= \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_{l'}, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2 \cdot \left( 1 + \sum_{s \geq s^*} \zeta_s(y) h_s(\langle \theta^*, z \rangle) \right) \right] \\ &\quad + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2] + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_l, v \rangle \langle w_{l'}, v \rangle \\ &\quad + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_l, v \rangle^2. \end{aligned}$$

As ψ(y, z)z is quadruple-integrable by [Assumption 4.1,](#page-6-2) the above integral is well-defined. We split the summation into two parts: l = l ′ and l ̸= l ′ . For l = l ′ , we directly have an O(L −1 ) bound for each direction θ ⋆ , v2, . . . , v<sup>d</sup> thanks to the polynomial-like tail property of ψ in [Assumption 4.1.](#page-6-2)

For l ̸= l ′ , [Lemma E.1](#page-32-1) implies that we have on the nice event Er(rϵ) that

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle w_l, w_{l'} \rangle| &\leq 4(\gamma^2 + 2\gamma\epsilon + \tilde{\epsilon}) \\ &\leq 8(\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon}) := \epsilon_2. \end{aligned}$$

Invoking [Lemma H.3,](#page-68-1) for any v ∈ {θ ⋆ , v2, . . . , vd}, it holds on Er(rϵ) that

$$\begin{aligned} & \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_l', z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2 \cdot \left( 1 + \sum_{s \geq s^*} \zeta_s(y) h_s(\langle \theta^*, z \rangle) \right) \right] \\ & \lesssim \epsilon_2^{s^*-1} \cdot \left( 1 + \frac{\epsilon_1^2}{\epsilon_2} + \left( \frac{\epsilon_1^2}{\epsilon_2} \right)^{s^*-1} \cdot \epsilon + \mathbb{1}(v \perp \theta^*) \cdot \left( \frac{\epsilon_1^2}{\epsilon_2} \right)^{s^*-2} \cdot \frac{\epsilon_2^2}{\epsilon_2} \cdot (\epsilon_1^2 + \epsilon_1 \cdot \mathbb{1}(s^* \geq 4)) \right) \\ & \quad (\text{E.10}) \end{aligned}$$

where we also define ϵ<sup>1</sup> := max{|⟨w<sup>l</sup> , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩|, |⟨w<sup>l</sup> ′ , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩|}, ϵ<sup>0</sup> := max{|⟨w<sup>l</sup> , v⟩|, |⟨w<sup>l</sup> ′ , v⟩|}. If the nice event E(ϵ) also holds, on which the following holds for all l ∈ [L]:

$$|\langle \xi_l, \theta^* \rangle| < \epsilon, \quad \max_{2 \leq i \leq d} |\langle \xi_l, v_i \rangle| < \epsilon,$$

then we have by [Lemma E.1](#page-32-1) that

$$|\langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle| \lesssim \gamma |\rho| + \epsilon, \quad |\langle w_l, v_2 \rangle| \lesssim \sqrt{1 - \rho^2} \gamma + \epsilon, \quad |\langle w_l, v_i \rangle| \lesssim \epsilon, \quad \forall i \geq 3, \quad \forall l \in [L].$$

Consequently, we can set ϵ<sup>1</sup> ≃ γ|ρ| + ϵ = o(1) and

$$\epsilon_0 \simeq \begin{cases} \gamma|\rho| + \epsilon, & \text{if } v = \theta^*, \\ \gamma\sqrt{1 - \rho^2} + \epsilon, & \text{if } v = v_2, \\ \epsilon, & \text{otherwise.} \end{cases}$$

Therefore, we have the ratio

$$\frac{\epsilon_2^2}{\epsilon_2} \simeq \frac{(\gamma|\rho| + \epsilon)^2}{4(\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})} \simeq \frac{\gamma^2|\rho|^2 + \epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon}}, \quad \frac{\epsilon_2^2}{\epsilon_2} \simeq \begin{cases} \frac{(\gamma|\rho| + \epsilon)^2}{8(\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})} \simeq \frac{\gamma^2|\rho|^2 + \epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon}}, & \text{if } v = \theta^*, \\ \frac{(\gamma\sqrt{1-\rho^2} + \epsilon)^2}{8(\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})} \simeq \frac{\gamma^2(1-\rho^2) + \epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon}}, & \text{if } v = v_2, \\ \frac{\epsilon_2^2}{4(\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})} \simeq \frac{\epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon}}, & \text{otherwise.} \end{cases}$$

Since ϵ <sup>2</sup> ≤ <sup>r</sup>ϵ, we can conclude that ϵ 2 <sup>1</sup>/ϵ<sup>2</sup> ≲ 1 and ϵ 2 <sup>0</sup>/ϵ<sup>2</sup> ≲ 1. Hence, the right-hand side of Eq. [\(E.10\)](#page-43-0) is bounded by ϵ s <sup>⋆</sup>−1 <sup>2</sup> ≃ (γ <sup>2</sup> + <sup>r</sup>ϵ) s <sup>⋆</sup>−1 for all v ∈ {θ ⋆ , v2, . . . , vd}.

Similarly, let us consider the term L −2 · P l̸=l ′ <sup>E</sup>Q[ψp1(y) 2 ]⟨w<sup>l</sup> , v⟩⟨w<sup>l</sup> ′ , v⟩. On the good event E(ϵ), we have

$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{L^2} \cdot \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_l, v \rangle \langle w_{l'}, v \rangle &\lesssim \epsilon_2^0 \cdot \mathbb{1}\{s^* \leq 2\} \\ &\lesssim \epsilon_2 \mathbb{1}(s^* \leq 2) \\ &\lesssim (\gamma^2 + \epsilon)^{s^*-1} \cdot \mathbb{1}\{s^* \leq 2\}. \end{aligned}$$

The first inequality holds because <sup>ψ</sup>p1(y) = 0 whenever <sup>s</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> ≥ 2 because of [Assumption 4.1\(b\)](#page-6-3) and the second inequality holds due to the condition that ϵ <sup>2</sup> ≤ <sup>r</sup>ϵ

Lastly for all the terms that take a single summation over l ∈ [L], we have them bounded by 1/L as each term in the summation can be upper bounded by 1. Combining the results for l = l ′ and l ̸= l ′ , we have on the event E(ϵ) ∩ Er(rϵ) that

$$\text{Var}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g_1, v \rangle] \leq \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g_1, v \rangle^2] \lesssim (\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})^{s^*-1} + \frac{1}{L}, \quad \forall v \in \{\theta^*, v_2, \dots, v_d\}.$$

Concentration. The first thing is to control the variation of g in the direction of θ ⋆ , where we need to upper bound the L r (<sup>P</sup><sup>θ</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> )-norm of ⟨g1, v⟩. To this end, we define G : (<sup>R</sup> × <sup>R</sup>) × <sup>R</sup> → <sup>R</sup> as

$$G(z, y, w) = |\psi(y, \langle w, z, \rangle) \cdot \langle z, v \rangle| + |\hat{\psi}_1(y) \cdot \langle w, v \rangle|.$$

Also we define the empirical measure dµ(w) = L <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup> P l δ(wl), then it holds by integral Minkowski's inequality that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle g_1, v \rangle^r]^{1/r} &\leq \left( \int d\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}(y, z) \left( \int d\mu(w) \cdot G(z, y, w) \right)^r \right)^{1/r} \\ &\leq \int d\mu(w) \left( \int d\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}(y, z) \cdot G(z, y, w)^r \right)^{1/r} \\ &\lesssim \frac{1}{L} \sum_l \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \cdot \langle z, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} + \frac{1}{L} \sum_l |\langle w_l, v \rangle|.\end{aligned}\tag{E.11}$$

For the second term, we have on E(ϵ) that |⟨w<sup>l</sup> , v⟩| ≤ 2γ + 2ϵ ≤ 1. Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have for the summand of the first term in Eq. [\(E.11\)](#page-44-0) that

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta,*}}[|\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \cdot \langle w_l, z \rangle|^r]^{1/r} \leq \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta,*}}[|\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle)|^{2r}]^{1/2r} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta,*}}[|\langle w_l, z \rangle|^{2r}]^{1/2r}.$$

Note that <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [|⟨w<sup>l</sup> , z⟩|<sup>2</sup><sup>r</sup> <sup>1</sup>/2<sup>r</sup> ≤ (2r−1)!!<sup>1</sup>/(2r) ≲ r 1/2 , it suffices to deal with <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [|⟨w<sup>l</sup> , z⟩|<sup>2</sup><sup>r</sup> 1/2r . To proceed, we can decompose ⟨w<sup>l</sup> , z⟩ to components that are correlated and independent with y as

$$\begin{aligned} \langle w_l, z \rangle &= \langle w_l - \langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle \theta^*, z \rangle + \langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle \langle \theta^*, z \rangle \\ &= \sqrt{1 - \langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle^2} \cdot x' + \langle w_l, \theta^* \rangle x' \end{aligned}$$

where x = ⟨θ ⋆ , z⟩ ∼ N (0, 1) is independent to x ′ = (1 − ⟨w<sup>l</sup> , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ 2 ) −1/2 · ⟨w<sup>l</sup> − ⟨w<sup>l</sup> , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩θ ⋆ , z⟩ ∼ N (0, 1) . Therefore, we define a Gaussian noise operator as Uρψ(y, x) = <sup>E</sup>x′∼N(0,1)[ψ(y, ρx + p 1 − ρ <sup>2</sup>x ′ )]. And it holds that

$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle)^{2r}] &= \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\mathbf{U}_{\langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle} \psi(y, x)^{2r}] \\ &= \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \mathbf{U}_{\langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle} \psi(y, x)^{2r} \cdot \frac{\mathbb{P}(x, y)}{\mathbb{Q}(x, y)} \right] \\ &= \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \psi(y, x)^{2r} \cdot \mathbf{U}_{\langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle} \left( \frac{\mathbb{P}(x, y)}{\mathbb{Q}(x, y)} \right) \right] \\ &\leq \left( \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y, x)^{4r}] \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \left( \mathbf{U}_{\langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle} \left( \frac{\mathbb{P}(x, y)}{\mathbb{Q}(x, y)} \right) \right)^2 \right] \right)^{1/2}, \end{aligned} \quad (\text{E.12})$$

where the second line follows from the property of the Gaussian noise operator in [\(B.2\)](#page-17-3). By assumption of the tail bound in [Assumption 4.1,](#page-6-2) we have that <sup>E</sup>Q[ψ(y, x) 4r ] ≤ Cp(4r) 4Cpr . For the second term, we have by the Parseval's identity that

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \left( \text{U}_{\langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle} \left( \frac{\mathbb{P}(x, y)}{\mathbb{Q}(x, y)} \right) \right)^2 \right] = 1 + \sum_{s \geq s^*} \langle \theta^*, w_l \rangle^2 \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\zeta_s(y)^2] \leq 2.$$

where we use the property that on the good event E(ϵ) we have |⟨w<sup>l</sup> , θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩| ≤ γ|ρ| + ϵ ≤ γ + ϵ < 1/2 and also <sup>E</sup>Q[ζs(y) 2 ] ≤ 1. And in conclusion, we get for the first term in Eq. [\(E.11\)](#page-44-0) that

$$\frac{1}{L} \sum_l \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \cdot \langle w_l, z \rangle|^r]^{1/r} \lesssim r^{C_p+1/2}.$$

Combining everything, we have

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\langle g_1, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} \lesssim r^{C_p+1/2}, \quad \forall v \in \{\theta^*, v_2, \dots, v_d\}.$$

Thus, by [Lemma J.3,](#page-82-1) there exists a {(z<sup>i</sup> , yi)}i∈[n] -measurable event E2,<sup>1</sup> with Pr(E2,1) ≥ 1 − d <sup>−</sup><sup>c</sup>/(MT) and it holds on E<sup>2</sup> that

$$\begin{aligned} & |\langle g, \theta^* \rangle - \mathbb{E}_{\theta^*} [\langle g, \theta^* \rangle]| \\ & \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\theta^*} [(g_1, \theta^*)^2] \cdot \log(d^c MT)}{n} + \frac{\log(d^c MT) \cdot \log(d^c MT n)^{C_p+1/2}}{n}} \\ & \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})^{s^*-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot \log(d)}{n} + \frac{\log(d)^{C_p+3/2}}{n}}, \quad \forall v \in \{\theta^*, v_1, \dots, v_{d-1}\}. \end{aligned} \quad (\text{E.13})$$

where we utilize the fact T, M, n all have polynomial dependency on d. Moreover, since we assume that

$$n = \Omega\left(((\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})^{s^\star - 1} + L^{-1})^{-1} \cdot \log(d)^{2C_p + 1}\right),$$

we have that the first term in Eq. [\(E.13\)](#page-44-1) dominates, which further implies that

$$|\langle g, \theta^\star \rangle - \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^\star}}[\langle g, \theta^\star \rangle]| \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})^{s^\star-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot \log(d)}{n}}.$$

Meanwhile, for the ℓ2-norm of g, we have by the Jensen's inequality that for any r ≥ 1,

$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\|g_1\|_2^r]^{1/r} &= \left( \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} \left[ \sum_{v \in \{\theta^*, v_2, \dots, v_d\}} \langle g_1, v \rangle^2 \right]^{r/2} \right)^{1/r} \\ &\leq \sqrt{d} \cdot \left( \frac{1}{d} \cdot \sum_{v \in \{\theta^*, v_2, \dots, v_d\}} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\|\langle g_1, v \rangle|^r] \right)^{1/r} \lesssim \sqrt{d} \cdot (r)^{C_p+1/2}. \end{aligned}$$

This polynomial tail bound enables us to apply [Lemma J.3](#page-82-1) for the ℓ2-norm of g, which implies that there exists some event E2,<sup>2</sup> with Pr(E2,2) ≥ 1 − d <sup>−</sup><sup>c</sup>/(MT), and it holds on E2,<sup>2</sup> that

$$\|g - \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[g]\|_2 \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \tilde{\epsilon})^{s^*-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot d \cdot \log(d)}{n}}.$$

Setting E<sup>2</sup> = E2,<sup>1</sup> ∩ E2,<sup>2</sup> gives the desired event. This concludes the proof of [Proposition E.4.](#page-33-0)

## F P R O O F O F T H E M A I N T H E O R E M F O R T H E S PA R S E P R I O R

### F. 1 P R O O F O U T L I N E A N D P R E L I M I N A R I E S

In this section, we provide a detailed proof for [Theorem 5.1.](#page-9-3) We begin with some good events that we will work with.

Signal concentration. We begin with a good event on which the signal spreads almost evenly within its support. Define a series of event:

$$\begin{aligned}\mathcal{E}_{0,r} &:= \{\|\theta\|_r^r \leq C_r \cdot k^{1-r/2}\}; \\ \mathcal{E}_{0,\infty} &:= \{\|\theta^\star\|_\infty \leq C_\infty \cdot k^{-1/2} \log(k)^{1/2}\}; \\ \mathcal{E}_{0,\sharp} &:= \left\{ \sum_{j \in [d]} \mathbb{1} \left\{ |\theta_j^\star| \geq \frac{1}{\sqrt{2k}} \right\} \geq \frac{k}{4} \right\}.\end{aligned}$$

The following lemma guarantees that the all the events above hold with high probability.

Lemma F.1 (Good signal). *Suppose that* k *is sufficiently large such that* k/ log(k) ≥ 32c(r ∨ 1)*,* k/ log<sup>r</sup>+2 k ≥ √ 2c + 2*, then it holds that*

$$\begin{aligned}\Pr(\mathcal{E}_{0,r}) \wedge \Pr(\mathcal{E}_{0,\infty}) &\geq 1 - O(k^{-c_{0,1}}); \\ \Pr(\mathcal{E}_{0,\sharp}) &\geq 1 - O(\exp\{-c_{0,2}k\}),\end{aligned}$$

*for some constants* c0,1, c0,<sup>2</sup> > 0*.*

*Proof of [Lemma F.1.](#page-45-2)* See [Appendix F.5.](#page-58-0)

For fixed s ⋆ , we collect all the indices r such that the corresponding nice event E0,r will be involved in the coming analysis. Define S(s ⋆ ) = s <sup>⋆</sup> − 1, s<sup>⋆</sup> − <sup>1</sup>{s <sup>⋆</sup> odd}, 2s ⋆ , 4s ⋆ . And we will stick to the following high probability event

$$\mathcal{E}_0 := \mathcal{E}_{0,\infty} \cap \mathcal{E}_{0,\sharp} \cap (\cap_{r \in S(s^*)} \mathcal{E}_{0,r}).$$

Preparation for characterizing one-step gradient. Following the same manner as the proof for the non-sparse case, we first characterize the alignment of the gradient step (without adversarial error term err (t) m,l,i). We begin with the definition of a minimal setup, that collects all the essential elements to form the one-step gradient. The following definition is the sparse analogue of [Definition E.2.](#page-32-2) Apart from the method of generating the noise, in the sparse case, we will analyze the gradient in a coordinate-wise manner to adapt to the sparse structure.

Definition F.2. *Fix* k*-sparse vectors* θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⊂ <sup>S</sup> <sup>d</sup>−<sup>1</sup> *with* ϕ = supp(θ) *and* ϕ <sup>⋆</sup> = supp(θ ⋆ )*. Let* ρ = ⟨θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩*. Suppose that a single batch of data* {(z<sup>i</sup> , yi)}i∈[n] *is i.i.d. generated from* P<sup>θ</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> *. We fix the index* m *as the current neuron. We first sample* ϕm,1, ϕm,2, . . . ϕm,L <sup>i</sup>.i.d. ∼ *Unif*(Sk,m)*, i.e., uniform distribution over all* k*-sparse supports with* m*-th index always included. Given these random supports, we sample independent noises* ξm,l ∼ *Unif*(<sup>S</sup> k−1 (ϕm,l)) *for* l ∈ [L]*. Now, for each* l ∈ [L] *and* γ = o(1)*, we define* wm,l = (γθ + ξm,l)/∥γθ + ξm,l∥2*. Then our target is*

$$\bar{g}_m = \frac{1}{nL} \sum_{i=1}^n \sum_{l=1}^L (\psi(y_i, \langle w_{m,l}, z_i \rangle) \cdot z_i - \hat{\psi}_1(y_i) \cdot w_{m,l}). \quad (\text{F.1})$$

*The associated good event is defined as*

$$\mathcal{E}_m(\epsilon) = \left\{ \sup_{l,j} |\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \leq \epsilon \right\}; \quad (\text{F.2})$$

$$\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_m = \left\{ \max_{l \neq l'} \left\{ \sup_{l'} |\phi_{m,l} \cap \phi_{m,l'}|, \sup_l |\phi_{m,l} \cap \phi^*|, \sup_l |\phi_{m,l} \cap \phi| \right\} \leq \log k \right\}. \quad (\text{F.3})$$

Almost orthogonality. Recall that our perturbation noise ξm,l is sampled from Unif(<sup>S</sup> k−1 (ϕm,l)), which is approximately isotropic. One can presume that each ξm,l is evenly distributed among different coordinates. Additionally, we expect that (ξm,l, ϕm,l) and (ξm,l′ , ϕm,l′ ) should have a negligible overlap. These two qualitative properties, which can help simplify the analysis, are captured by Eq. [\(F.2\)](#page-46-0) and Eq. [\(F.3\)](#page-46-1) in [Definition F.2.](#page-46-2) The following lemma characterizes the property of the perturbated weights wm,l on the nice event Em(ϵ) ∩ Er.

Lemma F.3 (Polarized weight on nice event, sparse case). *Consider the setting in [Definition F.2](#page-46-2) with* γ < 1/2*. Suppose that the nice event* Em(ϵ) ∩ Er<sup>m</sup> *holds and* ∥θ <sup>⋆</sup>∥<sup>∞</sup> ≤ 1/ log k*, then we have that*

$$\sup_{l,j} |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \leq 2(\gamma|\theta_j| + \epsilon);$$

$$\sup_l |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle| \leq 2(\gamma|\rho| + \epsilon).$$

*Additionally, we have that*

$$\sup_{l \neq l'} |\langle w_{m,l}, w_{m,l'} \rangle| \leq 4(\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log k).$$

*Proof of [Lemma F.3.](#page-46-3)* See [Appendix F.5.](#page-58-0)

This lemma, serving as the counterpart of [Lemma E.1,](#page-32-1) controls the behavior of the perturbated weight wm,l in its coordinates and alignment with θ ⋆ . Additionally, they are approximately orthogonal with each other, which allows for good characterization to the second moment of the gradient.

One may notice that the definition of E<sup>r</sup> differs from the non-sparse case, where we explicitly bound the correlation between different ξm,l. This is the benefit of the sparse structure, as two randomly sampled k-sparse supports are naturally of low overlap.

Before delving into the component-wise analysis, we begin with a proposition that will be frequently used to calculate the average contribution of each term in the gradient. This proposition serves as the counterpart of [Lemma H.4](#page-70-0) in the sparse case.

Proposition F.4. *Suppose that the polarization level* γ = o(1) *and the noise (*ξm,l, ϕm,l) ∼ *Unif* S k−1 (ϕm,l) ⊗ *Unif*(Sk,m)*. Let* ρ = ⟨θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ *where* θ *is the polarized direction in* wm,l*. Assume that the event* E0,s−1{<sup>s</sup> *odd*} *holds. Then we have that*

$$\mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^s] \simeq \begin{cases} \gamma \rho \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s-1})^{s-1} & \text{if } s \text{ odd;} \\ (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_s)^s & \text{if } s \text{ even,} \end{cases}$$

*Proof of [Proposition F.4.](#page-46-4)* See [Appendix F.5.](#page-58-0)

Recall that we define the good event over the signal as E<sup>0</sup> = E0,<sup>∞</sup> ∩ E0,♯ ∩ T r∈S(s <sup>⋆</sup>) E0,r, where S(s ⋆ ) = {s <sup>⋆</sup> − 1, s<sup>⋆</sup> − <sup>1</sup>{s <sup>⋆</sup> odd}, 2s ⋆ , 4s <sup>⋆</sup>}. This definition facilitates our defered analysis where we need to control multiple moments of different orders and we collect all the necessary good events in E<sup>0</sup> in the first place.

## F. 2 P R O P E R T I E S O F T H E G R A D I E N T S T E P

In this section, we preview some properties regarding the gradient defined in Eq. [\(F.1\)](#page-46-5). The following proposition deals with the first moment.

Proposition F.5 (First-order moment of the gradient). *Suppose that* θ ⋆ *is fixed such that the nice event* E<sup>0</sup> *holds. Under [Definition F.2,](#page-46-2) we choose* γ ≤ ϵ = o(1) *such that* Pr Em(ϵ) c ≤ O(k −s ⋆ ) *and*

$$L = \Omega \left( \log(d) \cdot (k \vee (\epsilon^{s^*-1} \cdot k^{s^*+1})) \right).$$

*Then there exists a* {ξm,l}l∈[L] *-measurable event* Em,<sup>1</sup> *with* Pr(Em,1) ≥ 1 − O(k <sup>−</sup>c<sup>1</sup> ) *for some constant* c<sup>1</sup> > 0*, such that on* Em,<sup>1</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) ∩ Erm*, it holds for any* j ∈ [d] *that*

$$\langle \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\bar{g}_m], e_j \rangle = \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s^*}(y) \cdot \widehat{\psi}_{s^*-1}(y)] \cdot \sqrt{s^*} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,t}}[\langle \tilde{w}_{m,t}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}]\theta_j^* + R_{m,j},$$

*where the expectation*

$$\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,l}}[\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] \simeq \begin{cases} \gamma \rho \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-2})^{s^*-2} \cdot \theta_j^* & \text{if } s^* \text{ is even;} \\ (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-1})^{s^*-1} \cdot \theta_j^* & \text{if } s^* \text{ is odd,} \end{cases}$$

*and* Rm,j *is the remainder that can be bounded by*

$$|R_{m,j}| \lesssim \left( (k^{-1} \vee (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*|)) \cdot (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*-1} + k^{-s^*} \right) \cdot |\theta_j^*| \\ + (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*} \cdot (\gamma|\theta_j| + k^{-1/2} \cdot (k/d)^{\{j \neq m\}/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)}.$$

*Proof of [Proposition F.5.](#page-47-1)* See [Appendix F.4.](#page-53-0)

The statement of this proposition clarifies the leading term explicitly, which enables us to track the leading term in the strong alignment more precisely. To complete this section, we provide a proposition that characterizes the fluctuation of the gradient, serving as the counterpart of [Proposition E.4.](#page-33-0)

Proposition F.6 (Fluctuation of mini-batch gradient). *Under the simplified setting [Definition F.2](#page-46-2) where* ψ *follows [Assumption 4.1.](#page-6-2) Additionally, suppose that the sample size*

$$n = \Omega\left(((\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log k)^{s^*-1} + L^{-1})^{-1} \cdot \log(d)^{2C_p+2}\right),$$

*where* C<sup>p</sup> *is the order of the polynomial tail in [Assumption 4.1\(c\).](#page-6-4) Then there exists a* {(z<sup>i</sup> , yi)}i∈[n]  *measurable event* Em,<sup>2</sup> *with* Pr(Em,2) ≥ 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>(c+1)/T)*, such that on* Em,<sup>2</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) ∩ Erm*, it holds that*

$$|\langle \bar{g}_m, e_j \rangle - \langle \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta,*}}[\bar{g}_m], e_j \rangle| \leq \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log k)^{s^*-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot \log(d)}{n}},$$

*for any* v ∈ {e1, e2, . . . , ed}

*Proof of [Proposition F.6.](#page-47-2)* See [Appendix F.4.](#page-56-0)

With these propositions, we have completed the preparation for the analysis of the gradient step and are ready to move on to the proof of the main theorem.

## F. 3 P R O O F O F T H E M A I N T H E O R E M

#### *Proof of [Theorem 5.1.](#page-9-3)*

Preparations. We first clarify the final good event that we will use throughout the proof. We first fix ϵ = k −1/2 · log k, then it holds by [Lemma J.6](#page-83-0) that for each m and t, we have

$$\Pr \left( \mathcal{E}_m^{(t)}(\epsilon)^c \right) \leq Ld \cdot O(\exp\{-ck\} + k^{-\log k/4}).$$

Since L and d are at most polynomials in k, we see that for sufficiently large k, it holds that Pr E (t) <sup>m</sup> (ϵ) c ≤ k −s ⋆ . Additionally, we see that γ = k −1/2 is fixed and our parameter configuration

$$n = \Omega\left((k \log^3 k)^{s^*} \cdot \log d\right), \quad L = \Omega(k^{(s^*+3)/2} \cdot \log(k)^{s^*-1})$$

are clearly compatible with the conditions in [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) and [Proposition F.6.](#page-47-2) At the t-th step, the mini-batch {(z (t) i , y (t) i )}i∈[n] , θ = θ (t) <sup>m</sup> and the error-free gradient

$$\bar{g}_m^{(t)} = \frac{1}{nL} \sum_{l=1}^L \sum_{i=1}^n \left( \psi(y_i^{(t)}, \langle w_{m,l}^{(t)}, z_i^{(t)} \rangle) \cdot z_i^{(t)} - \hat{\psi}_1(y_i^{(t)}) \cdot w_{m,l}^{(t)} \right) \quad (\text{F.4})$$

together form an instance of [Definition F.2,](#page-46-2) for which we can find the event E (t) m,1 and E (t) m,2 , both with probability at least 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>c−<sup>1</sup>/T), such that on E (t) m,<sup>1</sup> ∩ E(t) m,2 the results in [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) and [Proposition F.6](#page-47-2) hold.

The gradient we use in the algorithm differs from Eq. [\(F.4\)](#page-48-1) by the error term err (t) m,l,i · z (t) i . To control this difference, we define

$$\mathcal{E}_{m,3}^{(t)} = \left\{ \sup_i \|z_i^{(t)}\| \leq \sqrt{d} \right\}.$$

Given our specification on supm,l,i,t err (t) m,l,i, it holds on E (t) m,3 that for any v ∈ S d−1 :

$$\begin{aligned} |\|g_m^{(t)}\|_2 + \|\bar{g}_m^{(t)}\|_2| \vee |\langle g_m^{(t)}, v \rangle - \langle \bar{g}_m^{(t)}, v \rangle| &\leq \|g_m^{(t)} - \bar{g}_m^{(t)}\|_2 \\ &\leq \sup_i \|z_i^{(t)}\| \cdot \sup_l \text{err}_{m,l,i}^{(t)} \|z_i^{(t)}\|_2 \\ &\leq d^{-9s^*}. \end{aligned} \tag{F.5}$$

Our final event is fixed to be

$$\mathcal{E} = \mathcal{E}_0 \cap \bigcap_{m \in [d]} \bigcap_{t=1}^T \left( \mathcal{E}_m^{(t)}(\epsilon) \cap \tilde{\mathcal{E}}_m \cap \mathcal{E}_{m,1}^{(t)} \cap \mathcal{E}_{m,2}^{(t)} \cap \mathcal{E}_{m,3}^{(t)} \right).$$

With union bound, we have that Pr(E) ≥ 1 − O(d −c ) for some constant c > 0.

To avoid confusion, we denote for each m that gq (t) <sup>m</sup> = <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [g<sup>s</sup> (t) <sup>m</sup> ]. Later we will encounter some data dependent index mp and using gq (t) <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup> avoids the ambiguity of the expectation. With our choice of n and L, [Proposition F.6](#page-47-2) guarantees that for any m, t, j, it holds that

$$|\bar{g}_{m,j}^{(t)} - \check{g}_{m,j}^{(t)}| \lesssim k^{-(s^* - 1/2)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k. \quad (\text{F.6})$$

In the sequel, we will drop the superscript t whenever there is no ambiguity. We will frequently involve gr (t) <sup>m</sup> <sup>=</sup> <sup>P</sup>Topk(<sup>g</sup> <sup>m</sup> ) (g (t) <sup>m</sup> ).

Weak alignment. The proof towards the weak alignment in the initial step comprises three parts. In the first place, we will show that the index of the gradient we choose mp guarantees that |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| ≳ k −1/2 . Thereby, the corresponding gradient exhibits good alignment towards the signal. Based on this, we can show that the support we choose Top<sup>k</sup> (gm<sup>x</sup>) is of considerable quality by successfully identifying

ϕ ⋆⋆ = {j : |θ ⋆ j | ≥ 1/ √ 2k}. Combining these elements, we can show that the gradient grm<sup>x</sup> is well-aligned with the signal θ ⋆ .

We begin with analyzing the quality of gm<sup>x</sup> where m<sup>p</sup> = argmaxm∥grm∥2. With this objective in mind, we first work on deriving a signal-dependent upper bound for ∥grm<sup>x</sup>∥. Note that ρ<sup>m</sup> = ⟨θm, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ = |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup>|, where θ<sup>m</sup> = e<sup>m</sup> is the initial weight. Applying [Proposition F.5,](#page-47-1) we have for any ϕ, |ϕ| = k that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j \in \phi} |\tilde{y}_{\widehat{m}, j}|^2 &\lesssim (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*|)^{2 \mathbb{1}\{s^* \text{ even}\}} \cdot (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{2(s^* - 1 - \mathbb{1}\{s \text{ even}\})} \cdot \sum_{j \in \phi} \theta_j^{*2} \\ &\quad + (k^{-2} \vee (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*|)^2 \cdot (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{2(s^* - 1)} + k^{-2s^*}) \sum_{j \in \phi} \theta_j^{*2} \\ &\quad + (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{2s^*} \cdot k^{-1} \sum_{j \in \phi} \left( \theta_j^2 + \cdot (k/d)^{\mathbb{1}\{j \neq m\}} \right) + k^{-(2s^* + 1)} \end{aligned}$$

Note that we have ∥θ <sup>⋆</sup>∥<sup>∞</sup> ≲ k −1/2 · log k, it holds that

$$k^{-2} \vee (k^{-1} \cdot |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^\star|^2) \cdot (k^{-1/2} |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^\star| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{2(s^*-1)} \lesssim k^{-2s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{2s^*}.$$

For any ϕ such that |ϕ| = k, we have P j∈ϕ θ 2 <sup>j</sup> ≤ 1 for any θ such that ∥θ∥<sup>0</sup> = k. Therefore, we can further upper bound this quantity by

$$\begin{aligned} \sup_{|\phi|=k} \sum_{j \in \phi} |\tilde{g}_{m,j}|^2 &\lesssim (k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m,j}^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{2s^*-2} + k^{-2s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{2s^*} \\ &\quad + (k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m,j}^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{2s^*} \cdot k^{-1} \\ &\lesssim (k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m,j}^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{2s^*-2} \\ &\quad + k^{-2s^*} \cdot \log^{s^*/2} k + k^{-2s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{2s^*}. \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.7})$$

Now we combined Eq. [\(F.7\)](#page-49-0), Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2) and Eq. [\(F.5\)](#page-48-3) to conclude that

$$\begin{aligned} \sup_{|\phi|=k} \sum_{j \in \phi} |g_{\widehat{m},j}|^2 &\lesssim k \cdot \sup_j |g_{\widehat{m},j} - \widehat{g}_{\widehat{m},j}|^2 + k \cdot \sup_j |\widehat{g}_{\widehat{m},j} - \widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j}|^2 + \sup_{|\phi|=k} \sum_{j \in \phi} |\widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j}|^2 \\ &\lesssim k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot (|\widehat{g}_{\widehat{m}}^*| + k^{-1/2} \delta_+)^{2s^*-2} + k^{-2s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{2s^*}. \end{aligned}$$

By definition of grm, we can further conclude that

$$\begin{aligned} \|\tilde{g}_{\widehat{m}}\|_2^2 &= \sup_{|\phi|=k} \sum_{j \in \phi} |\langle g_{\widehat{m}}, e_j \rangle|^2 \\ &\lesssim k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*|^{2(s^*-1)} + o(k^{-2(s^*-1)}). \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.8})$$

On the other hand, for m ∈ ϕ ⋆⋆, we have (γ|ρ| + k −1/2 |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup>| + k −1 δ+) <sup>r</sup> ≳ k −r . Then it holds by [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) that

$$\sum_{j \in \phi^*} |\check{g}_{m,j}|^2 \gtrsim k^{-2(s^*-1)} - \tilde{O}(k^{-2s^*}),$$

and by Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2) and [\(F.5\)](#page-48-3), we have that

$$\|\tilde{g}_m\|_2^2 \gtrsim k^{-2(s^*-1)} - \tilde{O}(k^{-2s^*}). \quad (\text{F.9})$$

Now, combinig Eq. [\(F.9\)](#page-49-1) with Eq. [\(F.8\)](#page-49-2) by the definition of mp , we have that

$$k^{-(s^*-1)}|\theta_{\widetilde{m}}^*|^{2(s^*-1)} + o(k^{-2(s^*-1)}) \gtrsim k^{-2(s^*-1)} - \tilde{O}(k^{-2s^*}).$$

We conclude the first step from the last inequality that there exists a global constant c<sup>1</sup> > 0 such that for sufficiently large k:

$$|\theta_{\tilde{m}}^{\star}| \geq \left( c_1 \cdot (1 - o(1)) \cdot k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)} \cdot k^{s^{\star}-1} \right)^{1/2(s^{\star}-1)} \geq c'_1 k^{-1/2}. \quad (\text{F.10})$$

Now we move on to the support identification. We have shown that |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| ≳ k −1/2 . To establish ϕ ⋆⋆ <sup>⊂</sup> <sup>ϕ</sup><sup>p</sup> <sup>=</sup> Top<sup>k</sup> (gm<sup>x</sup>), it is sufficient to demonstrate that

$$\sup_{j \notin \phi^*} |g_{\hat{m},j}| \leq \inf_{j \in \phi^*} |g_{\hat{m},j}|. \quad (\text{F.11})$$

In the following, we will bound each side separately. Consider j /∈ ϕ ⋆ , we have by [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) that

$$|\widetilde{g_{\widehat{m},j}}| \lesssim (k^{-1/2}|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*} \cdot (k^{-1/2}|\theta_j| + (k/d)^{-1/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)}.$$

Combining this upper bound with Eq. [\(F.10\)](#page-49-3) and Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2) gives us that

$$\begin{aligned}
|g_{\hat{m},j}| &\leq |g_{\hat{m},j} - \tilde{g}_{\hat{m},j}| + |\tilde{g}_{\hat{m},j} - \tilde{g}_{\hat{m},j}| + |\tilde{g}_{\hat{m},j}| \\
&\lesssim (k^{-1/2} \cdot |\theta_{\hat{m}}^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{s^*} \cdot (k^{-1/2} |\theta_j| + (k/d)^{-1/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)} \\
&\quad + d^{-9s^*} + k^{-(s^*-1/2)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k \\
&\lesssim k^{-(s^*-1/2)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k + \tilde{O}(k^{-(s^*+1)}). \tag{F.12}
\end{aligned}$$

On the other hand, for j ∈ ϕ ⋆⋆, we have that

$$\begin{aligned} |\check{g}_{\hat{m},j}| &\gtrsim |k^{-1/2}\theta_{\hat{m}}^* + k^{-1}\delta_+|^{s^*-1} \cdot |\theta_j^*| \\ &\gtrsim k^{-(s^*-1/2)} \end{aligned}$$

Similarly, it holds that

$$\begin{aligned} |g_{\widehat{m},j}| &\gtrsim |\widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j}| - |g_{\widehat{m},j} - \widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j}| - |\widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j} - \widetilde{g}_{\widehat{m},j}| \\ &\gtrsim k^{-(s^*-1/2)} - o(k^{-(s^*-1/2)}). \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.13})$$

Comparing Eq. [\(F.12\)](#page-50-0) and Eq. [\(F.13\)](#page-50-1), we successfully validate Eq. [\(F.11\)](#page-50-2) holds, and consequently ϕ ⋆⋆ ⊂ ϕp.

We complete our proof of weak alignment by analyzing the inner product between grm<sup>x</sup> and θ ⋆ and the norm of grm<sup>x</sup> respectively. Since |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| ≳ k −1/2 , we have that

(γ|ρm<sup>x</sup>| + k −1/2 · |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| + k −1 δ+) s <sup>⋆</sup>−<sup>1</sup> <sup>∧</sup> (γ|ρm<sup>x</sup>| <sup>+</sup> <sup>k</sup> −1/2 · |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| + k −1 δ+) s <sup>⋆</sup>−2 · |γρm| ≳ k −(s <sup>⋆</sup>−1)/2 · |θ ⋆ mx| s <sup>⋆</sup>−1

For the inner product, we apply [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) for mp and each j ∈ ϕ ⋆⋆ and get that

$$g_{\widehat{m},j} \cdot \theta_j^* \simeq \theta_j^{*2} \cdot k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*|^{s^*} \cdot \text{sign}(\theta_{\widehat{m}}^*) \\ - (|R_{\widehat{m},j}| + |g_{\widehat{m},j} - \bar{g}_{\widehat{m},j}| + |\bar{g}_{\widehat{m},j} - \check{g}_{\widehat{m},j}|) \cdot |\theta_j^*|. \quad (\text{F.14})$$

Since ϕ ⋆⋆ ⊂ <sup>ϕ</sup>p1, we can lower bound the summation of the leading term as

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} \theta_j^{\star 2} \cdot k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\bar{m}}^*|^{s^*-1} &\gtrsim \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}^{\star*}} \theta_j^{\star 2} \cdot k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\bar{m}}^*|^{s^*-1} \\ &\gtrsim k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\bar{m}}^*|^{s^*-1} \cdot k^{-1} \cdot |\phi^{\star*}| \\ &\geq k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\bar{m}}^*|^{s^*-1}, \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.15})$$

where the last line holds by the definition of E0,♯ ⊂ E0. For the term associated with Rm,j <sup>x</sup> , we have by the characterization in [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} |R_{m,j}| \cdot |\theta_j^*| &\lesssim (k^{-s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{s^*} + k^{-s^*}) \cdot \sum_{j \in \phi^*} |\theta_j^*|^2 \\ &\quad + k^{-s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{s^*} \cdot k^{-1/2} \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} |\theta_j| \cdot |\theta_j^*| + |\theta_j^*| \cdot (k/d)^{\mathbb{1}\{j \neq m\}} \right) \\ &\quad + k^{-(s^*+1)} \cdot \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} |\theta_j^*|. \end{aligned}$$

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} |\theta_j^*| \cdot (|\theta_j^*| + (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}}) &\leq \left( \sum_{j \in [d]} |\theta_j^*|^2 \right)^{1/2} \cdot \left( \left( \sum_{j \in [d]} |\theta_j|^2 \right)^{1/2} + (1 + k^3/d^2)^{1/2} \right) \\ &= O(1). \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.16})$$

And therefore

$$\sum_{j \in \hat{\Phi}_1} |R_{m,j}| \cdot |\theta_j^*| \lesssim k^{-s^*} \cdot \log(k)^{s^*} + k^{-s^*} + \tilde{O}(k^{-(s^*+1/2)}). \quad (\text{F.17})$$

For the rest of the error terms, we have by Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2) and Eq. [\(F.5\)](#page-48-3) that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_1} (|g_{\hat{m},j} - \bar{g}_{\hat{m},j}| + |\bar{g}_{\hat{m},j} - \check{g}_{\hat{m},j}|) \cdot |\theta_j^*| &\lesssim \sum_{j \in \phi^*} |\theta_j^*| \cdot k^{-(s^*-1/2)} \cdot \log(k)^{-3/2} \\ &\leq k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot \log(k)^{-3/2}. \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.18})$$

Combining Eq. [\(F.14\)](#page-50-3), Eq. [\(F.15\)](#page-50-4), Eq. [\(F.17\)](#page-51-0) and Eq. [\(F.18\)](#page-51-1), we have that

$$|\langle \tilde{g}_{\widehat{m}}, \theta^\star \rangle| \gtrsim k^{-(s^\star - 1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\widehat{m}}^\star|^{s^\star - 1} - o(k^{-(s^\star - 1)}).$$

For the norm, we already have in Eq. [\(F.8\)](#page-49-2) that

$$\|\widetilde{g}_{\widetilde{m}}\|_2 \lesssim k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_{\widetilde{m}}^*|^{(s^*-1)} + o(k^{-(s^*-1)}).$$

Combining last two inequalities, we concludes that

$$\frac{|\langle P_{\hat{\phi}_1} g_{\hat{m}}, \theta^\star \rangle|}{\|P_{\hat{\phi}_1} g_{\hat{m}}\|} \gtrsim \frac{|\theta_{\hat{m}}^\star|^{s^*-1} - o(k^{-(s^*-1)/2})}{|\theta_{\hat{m}}^\star|^{s^*-1} + o(k^{-(s^*-1)/2})} = \Theta(1),$$

given that |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup><sup>x</sup>| ≥ c ′ 1k −1/2 for some constant c ′ <sup>1</sup> > 0. This concludes the proof of weak alignment.

Strong Alignment. Starting from the second step, we have by [Algorithm 2](#page-9-2) that all the neurons share the same weight parameter. Let θ be the weight parameter in any step after the first step and we suppose that ρ = ⟨θ, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ = Θ(1). From [Proposition F.5,](#page-47-1) we see that now the choice of the gradient should not change the quality of the gradient significantly, as γ|ρ| ≫ k −1/2 |θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup>| for any m ∈ [d]. Therefore, we start by analyzing the alignment increment using any gm. This alteration does not affect the alignment increment, but substantially simplifies the analysis.

We additionally define a support ϕ † = {j ∈ [d] : |θ ⋆ j | ≥ k <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup>}. In the following, we fix an arbitrary m ∈ [d]. We begin with analyzing the magnitude of gm,j for j ∈ ϕ † \ {m}. Since |ρ| = Ω(1), it holds that

$$(\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2} \cdot |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_{s^*-1})^{s^*-1} \simeq (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2} \cdot |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_{s^*-2})^{s^*-2} \cdot (\gamma|\rho|) \simeq k^{-(s^*-1)/2}.$$

With triangle inequality, [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) indicates that, for j ∈ ϕ † :

$$\begin{aligned}
|g_{m,j}| &\geq |\tilde{g}_{m,j}| - |\tilde{g}_{m,j} - \tilde{g}_{m,j}| - |\tilde{g}_{m,j} - g_{m,j}| \\
&\geq k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot |\theta_j^*| - k^{-s^*/2} \cdot |\theta_j^*| \\
&\quad - k^{-(s^*+1)/2} \cdot (|\theta_j| + (k/d)^{1/(j \neq m)}) - \tilde{O}(k^{-(s^*-1/2)}).
\end{aligned}$$

Similarly, we have for j /∈ ϕ ⋆ that

$$\begin{aligned}
|g_{m,j}| &\leq |\tilde{g}_{m,j}| + |\bar{g}_{m,j} - \tilde{g}_{m,j}| + |\bar{g}_{m,j} - g_{m,j}| \\
&\lesssim k^{-(s^*+1)/2} \cdot (|\theta_j| + (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}) + \tilde{O}(k^{-(s^*-1/2)}).
\end{aligned}$$

Comparing last two inequalities, we have that |θ<sup>j</sup> | > k−<sup>1</sup> implies that |gm,j | ≥ maxj /∈ϕ<sup>⋆</sup> |gm,j | for sufficiently large k, and therefore

$$\min_{j \in \phi^\dagger} |g_{m,j}| > \max_{j \notin \phi^*} |g_{m,j}|,$$

which means that ϕ † <sup>⊂</sup> <sup>ϕ</sup>p<sup>m</sup> <sup>=</sup> Top<sup>k</sup> (θm). Thereby, we have

$$\sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} |\theta_j^*|^2 \geq 1 - \sum_{j \notin \hat{\phi}^\dagger} |\theta_j^*|^2 \geq 1 - k^{-1}.$$

We then move on to the alignment analysis. Retreat to Eq. [\(F.23\)](#page-53-1), we define

$$\beta_m(\rho, \{\xi_{m,l}\}_{l \in [L]}, \theta^\star, \theta) = \frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s^\star}(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s^\star-1}(y)] \cdot \sqrt{s^\star} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,l}}[\langle w_m, l, \theta^\star \rangle^{s^\star-1}]}{\text{sign}(\rho)^{\mathbb{1}\{s^\star \text{ even}\}} \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^\star-1}};$$

$$r_{m,j}(\rho, \{\xi_{m,l}\}_{l \in [L]}, \theta^\star, \theta) = \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^\star}}[\langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle] - \theta_j^\star \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^\star-1} \cdot \text{sign}(\rho)^{\mathbb{1}\{s^\star \text{ even}\}} \cdot \beta_m. \quad (\text{F.19})$$

Then it follows from [Proposition F.5](#page-47-1) that whenever |ρ| = Ω(1), we have that β<sup>m</sup> > 0 and

$$\beta_m \vee \beta_m^{-1} < B$$

$$|r_{m,j}| \leq r_{m,j}^a + r_{m,j}^b,$$

where

$$\begin{aligned} r_{m,j}^a &\leq C_a k^{-(s^*+1)/2} \cdot (|\theta_j| + (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}); \\ r_{m,j}^b &\leq C_b \cdot |\theta_j^*| \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*} \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.20})$$

with some global positive constant B, C<sup>a</sup> and C0, whenever the designated parameters are compatible with the definition of our nice event. With this representation, we can deduce by Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2) and Eq. [\(F.5\)](#page-48-3) that

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle \tilde{g}_m, \theta^* \rangle| &= \left| \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} \langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle \cdot \theta_j^* \right| + \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} (|\langle g_m, e_j \rangle - \langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle| + |\langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle - \langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle|) \cdot |\theta_j^*| \\ &\geq \left| \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} \langle \tilde{g}_m, e_j \rangle \cdot \theta_j^* \right| - \sum_{j \in \phi^*} |\theta_j^*| \cdot O(k^{-(s^*-1/2)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k) \\ &\geq \beta_m \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*-1} \cdot \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} |\theta_j^*|^2 - \sum_{j \in \phi^*} |r_{m,j}| \cdot |\theta_j^*| - O(k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k) \\ &\geq (\beta_m \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*-1} - C_b \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*}) \cdot \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} |\theta_j^*|^2 - 2C_a \cdot k^{-(s^*+1)/2} - O(k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k). \end{aligned} \tag{F.21}$$

where the last inequality holds by Eq. [\(F.16\)](#page-51-2). To complete the analysis, we need an upper bound for ∥grm∥2. Note that by the triangle inequality and Eq. [\(F.19\)](#page-52-0), we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \|\tilde{g}_m\| &\leq \|P_{\hat{\phi}_m}\tilde{g}_m\| + \|P_{\hat{\phi}_m}\bar{g}_m - P_{\hat{\phi}_m}\tilde{g}_m\| + \|P_{\hat{\phi}_m}\bar{g}_m - P_{\hat{\phi}_m}g_m\| \\ &\leq \|\beta_m(\gamma|\rho)|^{s^*-1} \cdot P_{\hat{\phi}_m}\theta^*\| + \|P_{\hat{\phi}_m}r_m^a\| + \|P_{\hat{\phi}_m}r_m^b\| \\ &\quad + O(k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k), \end{aligned}$$

where r a <sup>m</sup> = (rm,1, . . . , r<sup>a</sup> m,d) ∈ <sup>R</sup> d and r b <sup>m</sup> = (rm,1, . . . , r<sup>b</sup> m,d) ∈ <sup>R</sup> d . To proceed, note that by Eq. [\(F.20\)](#page-52-1) and Eq. [\(F.6\)](#page-48-2), we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \|P_{\phi} r_m^a\| &\leq C_b k^{-(s^*+1)/2} \cdot (\|\theta\|_2 + \sqrt{1+k^2/d}) \\ &\leq 3C_b k^{-(s^*+1)/2}; \\ \|P_{\phi} r_m^b\| &\leq C_b \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*} \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} |\theta_j^*|^2 \right)^{1/2}. \end{aligned}$$

Putting these upper bounds together, we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \|P_{\hat{\phi}}g_m\|_2 &\leq (\beta_m \cdot (\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*-1} + C_b(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^*}) \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} |\theta_j^*|^2 \right)^{1/2} \\ &\quad + 3C_a k^{-(s^*+1)/2} + O(k^{-(s^*-1)} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k). \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.22})$$

Note that for any a<sup>1</sup> ∧ a<sup>2</sup> > b > 0, it holds that

$$\frac{a_1 - b}{a_2 + b} = \frac{(a_1 - b) \cdot (a_2 - b)}{a_2^2 - b^2} \geq (a_1/a_2 - b/a_2) \cdot (1 - b/a_2).$$

Setting ∆ = ·k <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup> + k −(s <sup>⋆</sup>−1)/2 · log−3/<sup>2</sup> k ∨ k <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup> = o(1), we get by combining Eq. [\(F.21\)](#page-52-2) and Eq. [\(F.22\)](#page-52-3) that

$$\begin{aligned} \frac{\langle \tilde{g}_m, \theta^* \rangle}{\|\tilde{g}_m\|} &\geq \frac{(\beta_m - C_b \cdot \gamma|\rho|) \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} \theta_j^{*2} \right) - 3C_a \cdot k^{-1} - O(k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k)}{(\beta_m + C_b \cdot \gamma|\rho|) \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} \theta_j^{*2} \right)^{1/2} + 3C_a \cdot k^{-1} + O(k^{-(s^*-1)/2} \cdot \log^{-3/2} k)} \\ &\geq (1 - O(\Delta))^{-1} \cdot \left( \frac{1 - C_b \beta_m^{-1} \gamma|\rho|}{1 + C_b \beta_m^{-1} \gamma|\rho|} \cdot \left( \sum_{j \in \hat{\phi}_m} \theta_j^{*2} \right)^{1/2} - O(\Delta) \right) \\ &\geq 1 - C \cdot k^{-1} - O(\Delta) \end{aligned}$$

where the last line holds because (1 − Ck−<sup>1</sup> ) <sup>r</sup> ≥ 1 − rC · k −1 for any C, r > 0 and sufficiently large k. Note that k <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup> = O(∆), we see that

$$\frac{\langle \tilde{g}_m, \theta^* \rangle}{\|\tilde{g}_m\|} \geq 1 - O(\Delta).$$

This concludes the proof of [Theorem 5.1.](#page-9-3)

#### F. 4 P R O O F O F T H E K E Y RE S U LT S

*Proof of [Proposition F.5.](#page-47-1)* First, by [Lemma H.2,](#page-67-0) we have for each j ∈ [d] that

$$\begin{aligned} \langle \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\bar{g}_m], e_j \rangle &= \sum_{s \geq s^*} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\zeta_s(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^s \cdot \langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle \\ &\quad + \sum_{s \geq s^*} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\zeta_s(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s}}{L} \cdot \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s-1} \cdot \langle \theta^*, e_j \rangle \cdot \\ &= \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\zeta_{s^*}(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s^*-1}(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s^*}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1} \cdot \langle \theta^*, e_j \rangle \\ &\quad + R_1 + R_2, \end{aligned} \tag{F.23}$$

where the remainders R1, R<sup>2</sup> are defined as

$$R_1 = \sum_{s \geq s^*} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s+1}(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_s(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^s \cdot \langle \theta^*, e_j \rangle;$$

$$R_2 = \sum_{s \geq s^*} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_s(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \cdot \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^s \cdot \langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle.$$

We also denote the leading signal term as

$$S = \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s^*}(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s^{*-1}}(y)] \cdot \frac{\sqrt{s^*}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^{*-1}} \cdot \langle \theta^*, e_j \rangle.$$

By definition R<sup>1</sup> collects the higher order term that aligns with the signal and the R<sup>2</sup> collects all the terms in the expected gradient that are parallel to wm,l. In comparison to the non-sparse case, here we are analyzing the gradient coordinate-wisely. Therefore, R<sup>1</sup> and R<sup>2</sup> need to be controlled separately. Analysis for the dominant term S in Eq. [\(F.23\)](#page-53-1). We first define

$$\tilde{w}_{m,l} = w_{m,l} \cdot \mathbf{1}\{|\sup_j \langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \leq \epsilon\}.$$

By definition of wrm,l, we have that wrm,l, l ∈ [L] are independent to each other and wrm,l = wm,l on event Em(ϵ). We can approximate the expectation of the ⟨wm,l, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ as

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,l}}[\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] &= \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1} \cdot \mathbf{1}_{\{ \sup_j |\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \leq \epsilon \}] \\ &\simeq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] \pm \Pr(\sup_j |\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| > \epsilon) \\ &\simeq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] \pm \Pr(\mathcal{E}_m(\epsilon)^c).\end{aligned}$$

Here the last line holds because Em(ϵ) <sup>c</sup> <sup>=</sup> <sup>∪</sup>l{sup<sup>j</sup> |⟨ξm,l, e<sup>j</sup> ⟩| > ϵ}. For the first term, it holds by [Proposition F.4,](#page-46-4) we have that on E0,s<sup>⋆</sup>−1−1{<sup>s</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> even}

$$\mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{s^*-1}] \simeq \begin{cases} \gamma \rho \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-2})^{s^*-2} & \text{if } s^* \text{ even;} \\ (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-1})^{s^*-1} & \text{if } s^* \text{ odd.} \end{cases}$$

For the second moment that is involved in the Bernstein's inequality, we have that on E0,2<sup>s</sup> <sup>⋆</sup>−2

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}[\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{2s^*-2}] &= \mathbb{E}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{2s^*-2} \mathbf{1}\{\sup_j |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \leq \epsilon\}] \\ &\leq \mathbb{E}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{2s^*-2}] \\ &\simeq (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_{2s^*-2})^{2s^*-2}\end{aligned}$$

To proceed, we have by Bernstein's inequality [\(Lemma J.1\)](#page-81-1) that there exists an event Em,<sup>11</sup> with Pr(Em,11) ≥ 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>cb,<sup>11</sup> ). And it holds on Em,<sup>11</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) that

$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{L} \sum_l \langle w_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle^{s^*-1} &= \frac{1}{L} \sum_l \langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle^{s^*-1} \\ &\simeq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}} [\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle^{s^*-1}] + E, \end{aligned}$$

where the error term E can be bounded by

$$|E| \leq (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_{2s^*-2})^{s^*-1} \cdot \sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{L}} + \frac{\epsilon^{s^*-1}\log(d)}{L} + \Pr(\mathcal{E}_m^c(\epsilon)).$$

Moreover, the assumption that

$$L \gtrsim \log d \cdot \left( k^2 \vee \left( \epsilon^{s^*-1} \cdot k^{s^*} \right) \right); \quad \Pr(\mathcal{E}_m(\epsilon)^c) \leq k^{-s^*};$$

allows us to simplify the upper bound for E, since

$$|E| \lesssim k^{-1} \cdot (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_{2s^*-2})^{s^*-1} + k^{-s^*}. \quad (\text{F.24})$$

In conclusion, we have on Em,<sup>11</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) that

$$S \simeq (\mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle^{s^\star-1}] + E) \cdot \langle \theta^\star, e_j \rangle.$$

We remain this form for further simplification.

Analysis for the first remainder R<sup>1</sup> in Eq. [\(F.23\)](#page-53-1). For any s, s′ , it holds by the property of likelihood ratio decomposition that

$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[|\zeta_s(y) \cdot \hat{\psi}_{s'}(y)|] &\leq \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_s(y)^2]^{1/2} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\hat{\psi}_{s'}(y)^2]^{1/2} \\ &\leq \sqrt{\sum_{s' \geq 0} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\hat{\psi}_{s'}(y)^2]}, \end{aligned}$$

and the last quantity is a constant that is independent to s, s′ . To bound the summation for s ≥ s ⋆ , we have on by [Lemma F.3](#page-46-3) that |⟨wm,l, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩| ≤ γ|ρ| + ϵ < 1/2 on

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{s \geq s^*} \frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^s &\lesssim \sum_{s \geq s^*} \sqrt{s+1} \cdot \left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{s-s^*} \cdot \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \\ &\lesssim \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*}. \end{aligned} \quad (\text{F.25})$$

Now it reduces to bound the right-hand side of Eq. [\(F.25\)](#page-55-0). Note that on Em(ϵ), wrm,l = wm,l. We can first track the first and second moment of ⟨wm,l, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ as

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,l}}[|\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*}] &\leq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*}] \\ &\leq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle^{2s^*}]^{1/2}.\end{aligned}$$

To bound the last quantity, we see that given E0,2<sup>s</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> , [Proposition F.4](#page-46-4) reads

$$\mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle^{2s^\star}] \leq (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^\star| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{2s^\star}.$$

By Bernstein's inequality, there exists a event Em,<sup>12</sup> with Pr(Em,12) ≥ 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>cb,<sup>12</sup> ) such that on Em,<sup>12</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ), it holds that

$$\frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^\star \rangle|^{s^\star} \lesssim \left(1 + \sqrt{\frac{\log d}{L}}\right) \cdot (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^\star| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^\star} + \frac{\epsilon^{s^\star} \log(d)}{L},$$

Given that L ≳ log(d) · k ∨ (ϵ s ⋆ · k s ⋆ ) , it further holds that

$$\frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \lesssim (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*} + k^{-s^*}.$$

In conclusion, it holds on Em,<sup>12</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) that

$$R_1 \lesssim \left( (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*-1} + k^{-s^*} \right) \cdot |\langle \theta^*, e_j \rangle|.$$

Analysis for the second remainder R<sup>2</sup> in Eq. [\(F.23\)](#page-53-1). Similar to Eq. [\(F.25\)](#page-55-0), we can first upper bound R<sup>2</sup> as

$$\begin{aligned} |R_2| &\lesssim \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \cdot |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| \\ &= \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L |\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \cdot |\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, e_j \rangle|, \end{aligned}$$

where the last line holds by the definition of Em(ϵ). We decouple the product with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as follows:

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{w}_{m,l}}[|\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \cdot |\langle \tilde{w}_{m,l}, e_j \rangle|] &\leq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{s^*} \cdot |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle|] \\ &\leq \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle|^{2s^*}]^{1/2} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle|^2]^{1/2}\end{aligned}$$

The first term in the upper bound can be tackled with [Proposition F.4.](#page-46-4) For the second term, we have that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle^2] &\lesssim \mathbb{E}([\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle + \gamma \cdot \langle \theta, e_j \rangle]^2) \\ &\lesssim \mathbb{E}[\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle^2] + \gamma^2 \theta_j^2 \\ &= \gamma^2 \theta_j^2 + \mathbb{E}[\mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l}\} \cdot \xi_{m,l,j}^2] \\ &\lesssim \gamma^2 \theta_j^2 + k^{-1} \cdot (k/d)^{\mathbb{1}\{j \neq m\}}.\end{aligned}$$

Here, the first line holds because ∥γθ + ξ∥<sup>2</sup> ≥ 1/2. The last line holds by applying [Lemma I.5](#page-76-2) and that <sup>P</sup>(j ∈ ϕm,l) ≤ k/d for j ̸= m. Note that each term in the summation of is bounded by ϵ log(k) up to a constant on Em(ϵ). We have by Bernstein's inequality [\(Lemma J.1\)](#page-81-1) that, there exists an event Em,<sup>13</sup> with Pr(Em,13) ≥ 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>cb,<sup>13</sup> ). And it holds on Em,<sup>13</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) that

$$|R_2| \lesssim \left( 1 + \sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{L}} \right) \cdot \left( \gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^\star| + k^{-1}\delta_+ \right)^{s^\star} \cdot (\gamma|\theta_j| + k^{-1/2}(k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}) + \frac{e^{s^\star+1} \log(d)}{L}.$$

Given that

$$L \gtrsim \log(d) \cdot \left( k \vee (\epsilon^{s^*+1} \cdot k^{s^*+1}) \right),$$

we conclude that it holds on Em,<sup>13</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) that

$$|R_2| \lesssim \left( \gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+ \right)^{s^*} \cdot (\gamma |\theta_j| + k^{-1/2} (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)}.$$

Summary of first-order moment. We now merge previous results to summarize the results for the first-order moment. Note that it is sufficient to set

$$L = \Omega\left(\log(d) \cdot (k \vee \epsilon^{s^*-1}(k \cdot \log k)^{s^*+1})\right)$$

Define the final event as Em,<sup>1</sup> = Em,<sup>11</sup> ∩ Em,<sup>12</sup> ∩ Em,13, which is {wm,l}l∈[L] measurable. By previous analysis, it holds on this event that

$$\begin{aligned} S &\simeq \theta_j^* \cdot (\gamma \rho)^{\mathbb{1}\{s^* \text{ even}\}} \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-1-\mathbb{1}\{s^* \text{ even}\}})^{s^*-1-\mathbb{1}\{s^* \text{ even}\}} + \theta_j^* \cdot E; \\ R_1 &\lesssim \left( (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+)^{s^*} + k^{-s^*} \right) \cdot |\theta_j^*|; \\ R_2 &\lesssim \left( \gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_+ \right)^{s^*} \cdot (\gamma |\theta_j| + k^{-1/2} \cdot (k/d)^{\mathbb{1}\{j \neq m\}/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)}. \end{aligned}$$

$$R_2 \lesssim \left( \gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^\star| + k^{-1}\delta_+ \right)^{s^\star} \cdot (\gamma|\theta_j| + k^{-1/2} \cdot (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}) + k^{-(s^\star+1)}.$$

Following the error term E in Eq. [\(F.24\)](#page-54-0), we define R = R<sup>1</sup> + R<sup>2</sup> + E, which be bounded by d

$$\begin{aligned}
 |R| &\lesssim \left( k^{-1} \vee (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*|) \cdot (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+)^{s^*-1} + k^{-s^*} \right) \cdot |\theta_j^*| \\
 &\quad + \left( \gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^*| + k^{-1}\delta_+ \right)^{s^*} \cdot (\gamma|\theta_j| + k^{-1/2} \cdot (k/d)^{1\{j \neq m\}/2}) + k^{-(s^*+1)}.
 \end{aligned} \tag{F.26}$$

And we summarize the first moment on Em,<sup>1</sup> ∩ Em(ϵ) as

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\langle \bar{g}_m, e_j \rangle] \simeq \theta_j^* \cdot (\gamma \rho)^{\mathbb{1}\{s \text{ even}\}} \cdot (\gamma |\rho| + k^{-1/2} |\theta_m^*| + k^{-1} \delta_{s^*-1-\mathbb{1}\{s^* \text{ even}\}})^{s^*-1-\mathbb{1}\{s \text{ even}\}} + R,$$
where  $R$  is upper bounded in Eq. (F.26).  $\square$ 

*Proof of [Proposition F.6.](#page-47-2)* Similar to the proof of [Proposition E.4,](#page-33-0) the proof of this proposition comprises two parts. To begin with, we calculate the variance of each coordinate of g<sup>s</sup>m.

Second moment calculation. It suffices to consider the variance of the first sample. To this end, we define

$$\bar{g}_{m,1} = \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L (\psi(y_1, \langle w_{m,l}, z_1 \rangle) \cdot z_1 - \hat{\psi}_1(y_1) \cdot w_{m,l}).$$

For any v ∈ {e1, e2, . . . , ed}, it holds by the definition of g<sup>s</sup>m,<sup>1</sup> that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle \bar{g}_{m,1}, v \rangle^2] &\lesssim \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l,l'=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_{l'}, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2] + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l,l'=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2 \langle w_l, v \rangle \langle w_{l'}, v \rangle], \\ &= \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_{l'}, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2 \cdot \left( 1 + \sum_{s \geq s^*} \zeta_s(y) h_s(\langle \theta^*, z \rangle) \right) \right] \\ &\quad + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \psi(y, \langle w_l, z \rangle) \langle z, v \rangle^2] + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_l, v \rangle \langle w_{l'}, v \rangle \\ &\quad + \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} [\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_l, v \rangle^2.\end{aligned}\tag{F.27}$$

In the same manner as the non-sparse case, we can derive a O(1/L) upper bound for the second and the last summation, which traverse through all l = l ′ . Recalling Eq. [Lemma F.3,](#page-46-3) we already have that

$$\begin{aligned} \sup_{l,j} |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| &\lesssim \gamma + \epsilon; \\ \sup_l |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle| &\lesssim \gamma |\rho| + \epsilon. \\ \sup_{l \neq l'} |\langle w_{m,l}, w_{m,l'} \rangle| &\lesssim \gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k). \end{aligned}$$

To incorporate with the notations in [Lemma H.3,](#page-68-1) we denote the upper bounds of the ⟨wm,l, e<sup>j</sup> ⟩, ⟨wm,l, θ<sup>⋆</sup> ⟩ and ⟨wm,l, wm,l′ ⟩(l ̸= l ′ ) as

$$\epsilon_0 = \gamma + \epsilon; \quad \epsilon_1 = \gamma|\rho| + \epsilon; \quad \epsilon_2 = \gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k),$$

respectively. By the virtue of [Lemma H.3,](#page-68-1) the desired expectation is behaving nicely if the ratio (ϵ 2 <sup>0</sup> ∨ ϵ 2 1 )/ϵ<sup>2</sup> is a constant term. To validate this fact, we note that

$$\frac{\epsilon_2^2}{\epsilon_2} \simeq \frac{\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k)}, \quad \frac{\epsilon_1^2}{\epsilon_2} \simeq \frac{\gamma^2 \rho^2 + \epsilon^2}{\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k)}.$$

Since ϵ ≪ 1 ≪ log(k) 1/2 , we conclude that ϵ 2 <sup>0</sup> ∨ ϵ 2 <sup>1</sup>/ϵ<sup>2</sup> ≲ 1 for sufficiently large k. Therefore, we have by [Lemma H.3](#page-68-1) that

$$\frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}} \left[ \psi(y_1, \langle w_{m,l}, z_1 \rangle) \cdot \psi(y_1, \langle w_{m,l'}, z_1 \rangle) \cdot \langle z_1, v \rangle^2 \cdot \left( 1 + \sum_{s=s^*}^{\infty} \zeta_s(y) h_s(\langle \theta^*, z \rangle) \right) \right] \lesssim \epsilon_2^{s^*-1}.$$

On the other hand, we have for the third term in Eq. [\(F.27\)](#page-57-0) that

$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{L^2} \sum_{l \neq l'} \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\hat{\psi}_1(y)^2] \langle w_{m,l}, v \rangle \cdot \langle w_{m,l'}, v \rangle &\lesssim \sup_l |\langle w_{m,l'}, v \rangle|^2 \cdot \mathbf{1}\{s^* \leq 2\} \\ &\lesssim \epsilon_0^2 \cdot \mathbf{1}\{s^* \leq 2\} \\ &\lesssim \epsilon_2 \cdot \mathbf{1}\{s^* \leq 2\}, \end{aligned}$$

where the first line holds by [Lemma F.3.](#page-46-3)

In summary, we have on the event Em(ϵ) ∩ Er<sup>m</sup> that

$$\sup_{v \in \{e_1, e_2, \dots, e_d\}} n \text{Var}_{\mathbb{P}_\theta}[\langle g_m, v \rangle] \lesssim \epsilon_2^{s^*-1} + \frac{1}{L} = (\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k))^{s^*-1} + \frac{1}{L}.$$

Concentration . We now turn to validate the condition of [Lemma J.3.](#page-82-1) For any v ∈ {e1, e2, . . . , ed}, we set <sup>G</sup>(z, y, w) = |ψ(y,⟨w, z⟩) · ⟨z, v⟩| <sup>+</sup> |ψp1(y) · ⟨w, v⟩| with the domain measure defined as dP<sup>θ</sup> <sup>⋆</sup> (z1, y1) × dµ(w), where dµ(w) = L <sup>−</sup><sup>1</sup> P l δ<sup>w</sup>m,l , the integral Minkowski's inequality implies

that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\langle g_{m,1}, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} &= \left( \int d\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}(y, z) \left( \int d\mu(w) |G(z, y, w)|^r \right)^{1/r} \right. \\ &\leq \int d\mu(w) \left( \int d\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}(y, z) |G(z, y, w)|^r \right)^{1/r} \\ &= \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\psi(y_l, \langle w_{m,l}, z_l \rangle) \cdot \langle z_l, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} + \frac{1}{L} \sum_l |\langle w_{m,l}, v \rangle|. \quad (\text{F.28})\end{aligned}$$

To proceed, we leverage Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to decouple the average of the product in the first term, which reads

$$\frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\psi(y_i, \langle w_{m,l}, z_i \rangle) \cdot \langle z_i, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} \leq \frac{1}{L} \sum_{l=1}^L \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\psi(y_i, \langle w_{m,l}, z_i \rangle)|^{2r}]^{1/2r} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\langle z_i, v \rangle|^{2r}]^{1/2r}.$$

Similar to the proof of [Proposition E.4,](#page-33-0) we have that

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\psi(y, \langle w_{m, l} z, \rangle)^{2r}] \leq \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[U_{\langle \theta^*, w_{m, l} \rangle} \left( \frac{\mathbb{P}(x, y)}{\mathbb{Q}(x, y)} \right)^2]^{1/2} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y, x)^{4r}]^{1/2} \lesssim r^{C_p 4r},$$

where the first inequality exactly repeats Eq. [\(E.12\)](#page-44-2) and the second inequality holds by [Assump](#page-6-4)[tion 4.1\(c\).](#page-6-4) On the other hand, we have that <sup>E</sup><sup>P</sup>θ<sup>⋆</sup> [⟨z<sup>i</sup> , v⟩ 2r <sup>1</sup>/2<sup>r</sup> ≤ r 1/2 . Since the second term in Eq. [\(F.28\)](#page-58-1) is bounded by O(1), we conclude that

$$\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}} [|\langle g_{m,1}, v \rangle|^r]^{1/r} \lesssim r^{C_p+1/2}.$$

Thus, [Lemma J.3](#page-82-1) implies that there exists a {(z<sup>i</sup> , yi)}i∈[n] -measurable event Em,<sup>2</sup> with probability at least 1 − O(d <sup>−</sup>c−<sup>1</sup>/T), on which for any v ∈ {e1, e2, . . . , ed}, it holds that

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle g_m, v \rangle - \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g_m, v \rangle]| &\lesssim \sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g_{m,1}, v \rangle^2] \cdot \log(d^{c+1}T)}{n} + \frac{\log(d^{c+1}T) \cdot \log(d^{c+1}Tn)^{C_p+1/2}}{n}} \\ &\lesssim \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k))^{s^*-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot \log(d)}{n} + \frac{\log(d)^{C_p+3/2}}{n}}, \end{aligned}$$

given that T, n are at most of polynomial rate in d. Since we assume that

$$n = \Omega\left(((\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k))^{s^*-1} + L^{-1})^{-1} \cdot \log(d)^{2C_p+2}\right),$$

the above inequality can be further simplified as

$$|\langle g_m, v \rangle - \mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{\theta^*}}[\langle g_m, v \rangle]| \lesssim \sqrt{\frac{((\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log(k))^{s^*-1} + L^{-1}) \cdot \log(d)}{n}}$$

Additionally, Em,<sup>2</sup> is the desired event. This concludes the proof of [Proposition F.6.](#page-47-2)

### F. 5 P R O O F S F O R TE C H N I C A L RE S U LT S I N T H E S PA R S E C A S E

*Proof of [Lemma F.1.](#page-45-2)* With slighly abuse of notation, we assume that θ <sup>⋆</sup> ∼ Unif(<sup>S</sup> k−1 ). We first consider the event E0,<sup>∞</sup> = {∥θ <sup>⋆</sup>∥<sup>∞</sup> ≤ C · k −1/2 log(k) <sup>1</sup>/<sup>2</sup>}. From the proof of [Lemma J.6,](#page-83-0) we see that

$$\mathbb{P}(\|\theta^*\|_\infty \geq t) \leq 2k \cdot \mathbb{P}(\theta_1^* \geq t) \leq 2k \exp(-k/16) + 2k \exp(-t^2 k/4).$$

Take t = C · k −1/2 log(k) 1/2 , we have that the failure probability is upper bounded by 2k exp(−k/16) + 2k 1−C <sup>2</sup>/4 .

For the r-norm, we leverage the property that θ <sup>⋆</sup> d. <sup>=</sup> Z/∥Z∥<sup>2</sup> where <sup>Z</sup> ∼ N (0, Ik). Now, ∥Z∥ 2 <sup>2</sup> = P <sup>i</sup>≤<sup>k</sup> Z 2 i , where Z 2 <sup>i</sup> − <sup>E</sup>[Z 2 i ] ≥ −1. Applying one-sided Bernstein's inequality with failure probability k −c<sup>0</sup> , we have that

$$\|Z\|_2^2 \leq k + \sqrt{2c_0 k \log(k)} + c_0 \log(k)/3.$$

On the other hand, note that we have for c<sup>1</sup> > 2 that

$$\mathbb{P}(\max_{i \leq k} |Z_i| > \sqrt{2c_1 \log k}) \leq k \cdot \mathbb{P}(|Z_1| > \sqrt{2c_1 \log k}) \leq k \exp\{-c \log k\} = k^{1-c_1},$$

To apply Bernstein's inequality, we note that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}[|Z_i|^r \cdot \mathbf{1}\{|Z_i| \leq \sqrt{2c_1 \log k}\}] &\leq \mathbb{E}[|Z_i|^{2r}]^{1/2}, \\ \mathbb{E}[|Z_i|^{2r} \cdot \mathbf{1}\{|Z_i| \leq \sqrt{2c_1 \log k}\}] &\leq \mathbb{E}[|Z_i|^{2r}],\end{aligned}$$

where <sup>E</sup>[Z 2r i ] = (2r − 1)!!. Therefore, it holds by truncated Bernstein's inequality that

$$\mathbb{P}\left(\|Z\|_r^r > (k + \sqrt{2c_2 k \log(k)}) \cdot \mathbb{E}[|Z_1|^{2r}]^{1/2} + (\sqrt{2C \log(k)})^r \cdot c_2 \log(k)/3\right) \leq k^{1-c_1} + k^{-c_2}$$

Combining the two bounds, we conclude that with probability 1 − O(k <sup>1</sup>−c0∨c1∨c<sup>2</sup> ), it holds that

$$\| \| {\boldsymbol \theta}^\star \|_r \stackrel{d.}{=} \frac{\| \| \| r \|_r}{\| \| \| r \|_2} \lesssim \frac{k + \sqrt{k \log k} + (\log k)^{1+r/2}}{(k + \sqrt{k \log k} + \log k)^{r/2}} \lesssim k^{1-r/2}.$$

with probability at least 1 − O(k −c ) for some constant c > 0.

We now move on to consider the event E0,♯ = P <sup>i</sup>≤<sup>k</sup> <sup>1</sup>{|θ ⋆ i | ≥ 1/ √ 2k} ≥ k/4 . First, it holds by the Hoeffding's inequality that

$$\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{i \leq k} \mathbb{1}\{Z_i^2 \geq 1/2\} - kp\right| \leq \frac{kp}{2}\right) \geq 1 - 2 \exp(-2p^2 k),$$

where p = <sup>P</sup>(Z 2 <sup>1</sup> ≥ 3/4) > 0.5. Denote above event as A1. On the other hand, we have by the Bernstein's inequality that

$$\mathbb{P}\left(\left|k^{-1} \sum_{i \leq k} Z_i^2 - 1\right| \leq 1/2\right) \geq 1 - 2 \exp\{-k/32\}.$$

Denote above event as A2. Then on the event A<sup>1</sup> ∩ A2, we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_{i \leq k} \mathbb{1} \left\{ \frac{Z_1}{\|Z\|} > \frac{1}{\sqrt{2k}} \right\} &= \sum_{i \leq k} \mathbb{1} \left\{ Z_i^2 > \frac{1}{2k} \sum_{i \leq k} Z_i^2 \right\} \\ &\stackrel{\text{A}_2}{\geq} \sum_{i \leq k} \mathbb{1} \{ Z_i^2 > \frac{3}{4} \} \\ &\stackrel{\text{A}_1}{\geq} \frac{kp}{2} > k/4. \end{aligned}$$

In conclusion we have that <sup>P</sup>(|{i : |θ ⋆ i | > 1/ √ 2k}| > k/4) ≥ <sup>P</sup>(A<sup>1</sup> ∩ A2) ≥ 1 − exp{−c3k} for some constant c<sup>3</sup> > 0.

*Proof of [Lemma F.3.](#page-46-3)* Clearly, it holds that

$$\|\gamma\theta + \xi_{m,l}\|_2 \geq \|\xi_{m,l}\|_2 - \gamma \cdot \|\xi_{m,l}\|_2 \geq 1/2.$$

Bu substituting this lower bound for the denominator, we have for any j, l that

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle w_{m,l}, e_j \rangle| &\leq 2(\gamma|\theta_j| + |\langle \xi_{m,l}, e_j \rangle|) \\ &\leq 2(\gamma|\theta_j| + \epsilon). \end{aligned}$$

The last line holds by the definition of Em(ϵ). On the other hand, we have for any l that

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle| &\leq 2(\gamma|\rho| + \sum_{j \in [d]} \xi_{m,l,j} \cdot \theta_j^* \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi^* \cap \phi_{m,l}\}) \\ &\leq 2\gamma|\rho| + 2\left(\sum_j \xi_{m,l,j}^2 \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi^* \cap \phi_{m,l}\}\right)^{1/2} \cdot \left(\sum_j \theta_j^{*2} \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi^* \cap \phi_{m,l}\}\right)^{1/2} \\ &\leq 2\gamma|\rho| + 2 \sup_j |\xi_{m,l,j}| \cdot \|\theta^*\|_\infty \cdot |\phi^* \cap \phi_{m,l}| \end{aligned}$$

To proceed, note that on the event Erm∩Em(ϵ), it holds that |ϕ <sup>⋆</sup>∩ϕm,l| ≤ log k and thatsup<sup>j</sup> |ξm,l,j | ≤ ϵ. Since we assume that ∥θ <sup>⋆</sup>∥<sup>∞</sup> ≤ 1/ log k, it holds that

$$|\langle w_{m,l}, \theta^* \rangle| \leq 2(\gamma|\rho| + \epsilon).$$

Now we turn to consider the correlation between wm,l and wm,l′ .

$$\begin{aligned} |\langle w_{m,l}, w_{m,l'} \rangle| &\leq 2 \left( \gamma^2 + \sum_j |\xi_{m,l,j}| \cdot |\xi_{m,l',j}| \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l} \cap \phi_{m,l'}\} \right. \\ &\quad + \gamma \sum_j |\theta_j| \cdot |\xi_{m,l,j}| \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l} \cap \text{supp}(\theta)\} \\ &\quad \left. + \gamma \sum_j |\theta_j| \cdot |\xi_{m,l',j}| \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l'} \cap \text{supp}(\theta)\} \right). \end{aligned}$$

For the second term, we have with the definition of Em(ϵ) that

$$\begin{aligned} \sum_j |\xi_{m,l,j}| \cdot |\xi_{m,l',j}| \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l} \cap \phi_{m,l'}\} &\leq \max_{j,l} |\xi_{m,l,j}|^2 \cdot |\phi_{m,l} \cap \phi_{m,l'}| \\ &\leq \epsilon^2 \log k. \end{aligned}$$

For the third term, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

$$\gamma \sum_j |\theta_j| \cdot |\xi_{m,v',j}| \cdot \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_{m,l} \cap \phi_{m,v'}\} \leq \gamma \|\theta\|_2 \cdot \epsilon \sqrt{\log k} = \leq \gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log k.$$

Putting them together, we have that

$$|\langle w_{m,l}, w_{m,l'} \rangle| \leq 4(\gamma^2 + \epsilon^2 \log k).$$

This concludes the proof of [Lemma F.3.](#page-46-3)

*Proof of [Proposition F.4.](#page-46-4)* For conciseness, we momentarily drop the subscript m, l in the following analysis. Conditioning on fixed ϕ, we have that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_w[\langle w, \theta^* \rangle^s] &= \mathbb{E}_w[\|\gamma\theta + \xi\|_2^{-s} \cdot (\gamma\langle\theta, \theta^*\rangle + \langle\xi, \theta^*\rangle)^s] \\ &= \mathbb{E}_\phi[\mathbb{E}_w[\|\gamma\theta + \xi\|_2^{-s} \cdot (\gamma\rho + \langle\xi, P_\phi\theta^*\rangle)^s \mid \phi]].\end{aligned}\tag{F.29}$$

Given the polarization level γ = o(1), we see that ∥γθ +ξ∥ s+1 <sup>2</sup> ≃ 1±o(1), and it suffices to evaluate E<sup>w</sup> γρ + ⟨ξ, Pϕθ ⋆ ⟩ s  ϕ . Without loss of generality, we assume that 1 ∈ ϕ and we can translate Pϕθ ⋆ into the first coordinate by the isotropy of ξ over S k−1 (ϕ). To this end, we can characterize the first term as follows:

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}[(\gamma\rho + \langle \xi, P_\phi \theta^\star \rangle)^s \mid \phi] &= \mathbb{E}[(\gamma\rho + \langle \xi, \|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2 \cdot e_1)^s \mid \phi] \\ &= \sum_{r=0}^s \binom{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor}{r} (\gamma\rho)^{s-r} \cdot \|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2^r \cdot \mathbb{E}[\xi_1^r \mid \phi] \cdot \mathbf{1}\{r \text{ even}\} \\ &\stackrel{\text{i)}}{\simeq} \sum_{r=0}^{\lfloor s/2 \rfloor} \binom{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor}{2r} (\gamma\rho)^{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor - 2r} \cdot \|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2^{2r} \cdot k^{-r} \cdot (\gamma\rho)^{\mathbf{1}\{s \text{ odd}\}} \\ &= (\gamma\rho)^{\mathbf{1}\{s \text{ odd}\}} \cdot ((\gamma\rho + k^{-1/2}\|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2)^{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor} + (\gamma\rho - k^{-1/2}\|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2)^{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor})/2 \\ &\simeq (\gamma\rho)^{\mathbf{1}\{s \text{ odd}\}} \cdot (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}\|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2)^{2\lfloor s/2 \rfloor}. \quad (\text{F.30})\end{aligned}$$

Here, (i) holds by applying [Lemma I.5](#page-76-2) and ≃ denotes the equality that is up to a s-dependent multiplicative constant.

$$\mathbb{E}_w[\langle w, \theta^\star \rangle^s \mid \phi] \simeq (\gamma\rho)^{1\{s \text{ odd}\}} (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2} \|P_\phi \theta^\star\|_2)^{s-1\{s \text{ odd}\}}. \quad (\text{F.31})$$

In the sequel, we consider averaging over ϕ. From Eq. [\(F.31\)](#page-60-2), we see that it suffices to consider <sup>E</sup>ϕ[(γ|ρ| + k <sup>−</sup>1/<sup>2</sup>∥Pϕθ <sup>⋆</sup>∥2) r ] for some r ≥ 2. We alter the notation to facilitate some deferred calculation. Consider m ⊂ [d] with constant size |m| = O(1) that does not scale with k or d. Now define ϕ<sup>m</sup> ∼ Unif{Sk,m}, where Sk,<sup>m</sup> = {S ⊂ [d] : |S| = k, m ⊂ S}. It is easily seen that this definition covers previous definition of Sk,m by setting m = {m}. We characterize the magnitude of <sup>E</sup>ϕm[∥Pϕmθ ⋆∥ r 2 ] from both sides as follows. For the lower bound, we have that

$$\begin{aligned}\mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}[\|P_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\theta^*\|_r^r] &= \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\left[(\|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_2^2 + \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^2 \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_{\mathbf{m}}\})^{r/2}\right] \\ &\geq \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\left[\|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^r \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\right] \\ &\stackrel{(i)}{\simeq} (1 - k/d) \cdot \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \frac{k}{d} \cdot \|\theta^*\|_r^r \\ &\stackrel{(ii)}{\simeq} \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \frac{k}{d} \cdot k^{1-r/2} \cdot \|\theta^*\|_2^{r/2} \\ &= \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \frac{k^2}{d} \cdot k^{-r/2}.\end{aligned}$$

Here (i) holds by the fact that <sup>E</sup>[1{j ∈ ϕm}] ≃ k/d for j /∈ m, and (ii) is a consequence of Jensen's inequality. For the upper bound, we have that

$$\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}_{\phi_m}[\|P_\phi \theta^*\|_2^r] &= \mathbb{E}_{\phi_m}\left[\left(\|\theta_m^*\|_2^2 + \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^2 \cdot \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_m\}\right)^{r/2}\right] \\ &\lesssim \mathbb{E}_{\phi_m}\left[\|\theta_m^*\|_r^r + \left(\underbrace{\sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^2 \cdot \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_m\}}_{|(\phi_m \cap \phi^*) \setminus \{m\}| \text{ nonzero summands}}\right)^{r/2}\right] \\ &\stackrel{\text{Jensen}}{\lesssim} \|\theta_m^*\|_r^r + \mathbb{E}_{\phi_m}\left[|(\phi_m \cap \phi^*) \setminus \mathbf{m}|^{r/2-1} \cdot \left(\sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^r \cdot \mathbb{1}\{j \in \phi_m\}\right)\right]. \quad (\text{F.32}) \end{aligned}$$

Next, we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as follows:

$$\begin{aligned}
(\text{F.32}) &= \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}} \left[ \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^r \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}^2 \cdot |(\phi_{\mathbf{m}} \cap \phi^*) \setminus \mathbf{m}|^{r/2-1} \right] \\
&\leq \|\theta_m^*\|_r^r + \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}} \left[ \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^{2r} \cdot \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{\mathbf{m}}\} \right]^{1/2} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}} \left[ \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} \mathbf{1}\{j \in \phi_{\mathbf{m}}\} \cdot |(\phi_{\mathbf{m}} \cap \phi^*) \setminus \mathbf{m}|^{r-2} \right]^{1/2} \\
&= \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \left( \frac{k}{d} \cdot \sum_{j \notin \mathbf{m}} |\theta_j^*|^{2r} \right)^{1/2} \cdot \mathbb{E}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}} [|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}} \cap \phi^*) \setminus \mathbf{m}|^{r-1}]^{1/2} \\
&\stackrel{(i)}{\lesssim} \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \left( \frac{k}{d} \cdot k^{1-r} \right)^{1/2} \cdot \left( \frac{k^2}{d} \right)^{(r-1)/2} \\
&= \|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^*\|_r^r + \frac{k^2}{d} \cdot k^{-r/2},
\end{aligned}$$

where (i) holds by E0,2<sup>r</sup> and [Lemma J.7.](#page-83-1) In conclusion, we have that <sup>E</sup>ϕm[∥Pϕθ ⋆∥ r ] ≃ ∥θ ⋆ <sup>m</sup>∥ r <sup>r</sup> + k −r/2 · δ given that k = o( √ d). Combining this result with Eq. [\(F.31\)](#page-60-2), we obtain that

$$\mathbb{E}_w[\langle w, \theta^\star \rangle^s] \simeq (\gamma\rho)^{1\{s \text{ odd}\}} (\gamma|\rho| + k^{-1/2}|\theta_m^\star| + k^{-1}\delta_{1/(s-1\{s \text{ odd}\})})^{s-1\{s \text{ odd}\}}$$

where δ = k <sup>2</sup>/d = o(1) and δ<sup>r</sup> = δ 1/r .
